О sages, the one who does not maintain properly his parents, teachers, daughter of the teachers and orphan sister or daughter or the relatives who are without any protection because of miserliness and is always engaged in collecting of wealth, such a house is never to be visited by me. Such of the people as ha^e dirty teeth and clothes, dry heads, the one whose mouth get deformed at the time of speaking or laughing, I do not visit the place of such people.

## \|\| 23.34 \|\|

*adhautapādaśāyī yo nagnaḥ śete'tinidritaḥ \|*  
*sandhyāśāyī divāśāyī yāmi tasya na mandiram \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Such foolish people who live near the droppings of refuse and urine, the one who sleeps with wet feet, I do not visit the abodes of such people.

## \|\| 23.35 \|\|

*mūrdhni tailaṃ puro dattvā yo'nyadaṅgamupaspṛśet \|*  
*dadāti paścādgātre vā yāmi tasya na mandiram \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. The one who sleeps without washing the feet or sleeps getting unrobed or the one who sleeps during the day time or the evening, I do not visit the houses of such people. The one who first applies oil on the head and then on the entire body, I do not visit the place of such people.

## \|\| 23.36 \|\|

*dattvā tailaṃ mūrdhni gātre viṇmūtraṃ yaḥ samutsṛjet \|*  
*praṇamedāharetpuṣpaṃ yāmi tasya na mandiram \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Those who after applying oil on the body, drop refuse or ease themselves or bow in reverence or picks up flowers, I do not visit the houses of such people.

## \|\| 23.37 \|\|

*tṛṇaṃ chinatti nakharairnakharairvilikhenmahīm \|*  
*gātre pāde malaṃ yasya yāmi tasya na mandiram \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. The one who breaks the straw with nails and digs the earth with his nails, the one whose body and the feet always remain dirty, I do not visit the house of such people.

## \|\| 23.38-39 \|\|

*svadattāṃ paradattāṃ vā brahmavṛttiṃ surasya ca \|*  
*yo harejjñānaśīlaśca yāmi tasya na mandiram \|\| 38 \|\|*

*yatkarma dakṣiṇāhīnaṃ kurute mūḍhadhīḥ śaṭhaḥ \|*  
*sa pāpī puṇyahīnaśca yāmi tasya na mandiram \|\| 39 \|\|*

38-39. The one who being all knowledgeable, usurps the property of others, the houses of such people are never visited by me. Such of the foolish persons who are wicked and perform sacrifice without offering daksina, they are deprived of all the merits and I do not visit the houses of such sinful people.

## \|\| 23.40 \|\|

*mantravidyopajīvī ca grāmayājī cikitsakaḥ \|*  
*sūpakṛddevalaścaiva yāmi tasya na mandiram \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. The one who lives on the application of the knowledge of mantra, the one who performs yajna from village to village, is the vaidyas, the store-keepers and temple priests, I never visit the houses of such people.

## \|\| 23.41 \|\|

*vivāhaṃ dharmakāryaṃ vā yo nihanti ca kopataḥ \|*  
*divāmaithunakārī yo yāmi tasya na mandiram \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. The one who destroys some religious ceremony including the performing of marriage in anger or the one who indulges in love-sport during the day time, I do not visit the places of such people.

## \|\| 23.42 \|\|

*ityuktvā sā mahālakṣmīrantarddhānaṃ jagāma ha \|*  
*dadau dṛṣṭiṃ ca devānāṃ gṛhe martye ca nārada \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. О Narada, thus speaking Maha-Lakshmi disappeared from the scene and started gazing at the houses of the gods and the humans.

## \|\| 23.43-44 \|\|

*tāṃ praṇamya surāḥ sarve munayaśca mudā'nvitāḥ \|*  
*prajagmuḥ svālayaṃ śīghraṃ śatrutyaktaṃ suhṛdyutam \|\| 43 \|\|*

*nedurdundubhayaḥ svarge babhūvuḥ puṣpavṛṣṭayaḥ \|*  
*prāpurdevāḥ svarājyaṃ ca niścalāṃ kamalāṃ mune \|\| 44 \|\|*

43-44. All the gods, sages then bowed in reverence to her delightfully and went back to their respective abodes which had been relieved of the enemies and were filled with friends. О sage, the gods got back their kingdoms and the everlasting Lakshmi. The drums were beaten in heaven and flowers were rained.

## \|\| 23.45 \|\|

*ityevaṃ kathitaṃ vatsa lakṣmīcaritamuttamam \|*  
*sukhadaṃ mokṣadaṃ sāraṃ kiṃ punaḥ śrotumicchasi \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. О son, thus I have narrated to you the noble story of goddess Lakshmi who bestows all the pleasures and salvation and is the essence of all. What else do you want to listen to from me?

## Chapter 24

## \|\| 24.1 \|\|

*nārada uvāca \|*  
*nārāyaṇa mahābhāga hareraṃśasamudbhava \|*  
*sarvaṃ śrutaṃ tvatprasādādgaṇeśacaritaṃ śubham \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Narada said – О Narayana, О virtuous one, you are born out of the Arhsa of the lord; I have listened to the entire story by your grace, about Ganesha.

## \|\| 24.2-3 \|\|

*dantadvayayutaṃ vaktraṃ gajarājasya bālake \|*  
*viṣṇunā yojitaṃ brahmannekadantaḥ kathaṃ śiśuḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

*kuto gato'sya danto'nyastadbhavānvaktumarhati \|*  
*sarveśvarastvaṃ sarvajñaḥ kṛpāvānbhaktavatsalaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

2-3. О Brahman, the king of the elephant had two tusks which was fixed on the trunk of the child but how was the child left with only one tusk. Where did the other tusk disappear. You kindly tell me because you are the lord of all and are quite compassionate, besides being the beloved of the devotees and are all knowledgeable.

## \|\| 24.4-5 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*nāradasya vacaḥ śrutvā smerānana saroruhaḥ \|*  
*ekadantasya caritaṃ pravaktumupacakrame \|\| 4 \|\|*

*nārāyaṇa uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu nārada vakṣye'hamitihāsaṃ purātanam \|*  
*ekadantasya caritaṃ sarvamaṃgalamaṃgalam \|\| 5 \|\|*

4-5. Suta said – On hearing the words of Narada, the lord smiled and started narrating the story about Ganesha having a single tusk which is part of ancient history and provides welfare to all the welfare.

## \|\| 24.6-7 \|\|

*ekadā kārttavīryyaśca jagāma mṛgayāṃ mune \|*  
*mṛgānnihatya bahulānpariśrānto babhūva saḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

*niśāmukhe dine'tīte tatra tasthau vane nṛpaḥ \|*  
*jamadagnyāśramābhyāśe copoṣyānīkasaṃyutaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

6-7. О sage, once the king Kartavirya went for hunting in the forest and felt tired after hunting a number of deer. It was already the evening time and the king camped in the forest together with his army. Close by, there was a hermitage of sage Jamadagni. The king was quite unaware of the same and had to fast for the night.

## \|\| 24.8 \|\|

*prātaḥ sarovare rājā snātaḥ śuciralaṃkṛtaḥ \|*  
*tadā''treyeṇa dattaṃ ca hyajapadbhaktito manum \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. In the early morning, the king took a bath and getting purified, he adorned himself with ornaments and recited the mantra of DattStreya with devotion.

## \|\| 24.9-11 \|\|

*munirdadarśa rājānaṃ śuṣkakaṇṭhauṣṭhatālukam \|*  
*prītyā''dareṇa mṛdulaṃ papraccha kuśalaṃ muniḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

*nanāma sambhamādrājā muniṃ sūryyasamaprabham \|*  
*sa ca tasmai dadau prītyā praṇatāya śubhāśiṣaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

*vṛttāntaṃ kathayāmāsa rājā cānaśanādikam \|*  
*saṃbhrameṇaiva muninā trasto rājā nimantritaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

9-11. Thereafter, the sage spotted the king and he felt panicky. His lips, throat and palate dried up. Lovingly with his tender voice, he enquired respectfully about the welfare of the king. The king bowed in reverence to the sage who was shining like the sun. The sage on his part offered blessings to the humble king. Thereafter the king narrated the story of his fasting during the night to the sage, hearing which the sage getting panicky invited the king for food in his hermitage.

## \|\| 24.12 \|\|

*vijñāpya taṃ muniśreṣṭhaḥ prayayau svālayaṃ mudā \|*  
*etadvṛttaṃ kāmadhenuṃ kathayāmāsa bhītavat \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The sage lived in his hermitage and narrated the story feeling panicky to Kamadhenu, the sacred cow.

## \|\| 24.13 \|\|

*uvāca sā muniṃ bhītaṃ bhayaṃ kiṃ te mama sthitau \|*  
*jagadbhojayituṃ śaktastvaṃ mayā ko nṛpo mune \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. She spoke to the panicky sage, "O sage, why should you be afraid of anything, when I am there." You are competent enough to serve food to the entire universe through me, what to speak of a king.

## \|\| 24.14-23 \|\|

*rājabhojanayogyārhaṃ yadyaddravyaṃ prayācase \|*  
*sarvaṃ tubhyaṃ pradāsyāmi triṣu lokeṣu durlabham \|\| 14 \|\|*

*sauvarṇāni ca raupyāṇi pātrāṇi vividhāni ca \|*  
*bhojanārhāṇyasaṃkhyāni pākapātrāṇi yāni ca \|\| 15 \|\|*

*pātrāṇi svādupūrṇāni pradadau munaye ca sā \|\| 16 \|\|*

*nānāvidhāni svādūni paripakvaphalāni ca \|*  
*panasāmraśrīphalāni nārikelādikāni ca \|\| 17 \|\|*

*rāśībhūtānyasaṃkhyāni svādulaḍḍukarāśayaḥ \|*  
*yavagodhūmacūrṇānāṃ bhakṣyāṇi vividhāni ca \|\| 18 \|\|*

*pakvānnānāṃ parvatāṃśca paramānnasya kandarān \|*  
*dugdhānāṃ ca ghṛtānāṃ ca nadīrdadhnāṃ dadau mudā \|\| 19 \|\|*

*śarkarāṇāṃ tathā rāśiṃ modakānāṃ ca parvatān \|*  
*pṛthukānāṃ suśālīnāṃ parvatānpradadau mudā \|\| 20 \|\|*

*tāmbūlaṃ ca dadau pūrṇaṃ karpūrādisuvāsitam \|*  
*nṛpayogyaṃ kautukācca sundaraṃ vastrabhūṣaṇam \|\| 21 \|\|*

*muniḥ sambhṛtasambhāro dattvā dravyaṃ manoharam \|*  
*bhojayāmāsa rājānaṃ sasainyamapi līlayā \|\| 22 \|\|*

*yadyastu durlabhaṃ vastu paripūrṇaṃ nṛpeśvara \|*  
*jagāma vismayaṃ rājā dṛṣṭvā pātrāṇyuvāca ha \|\| 23 \|\|*

14-23. Whatever food stuffs and royal delicious dishes will be desired, you shall be given by me. These will be made available, though they will be difficult to get in the three worlds. Several of the vases of gold and silver, innumerable cooking vases, the betel boxes made of pure gems and delicious food stuffs in the fruits including jack-fruit, mango, wood apple, coconut and delicious sweet balls in heaps, special preparation of wheat and barley flour, heaps of fried sweets, caves filled with sacred food, the milk, curd and ghee flowing in streams were given by the sacred cow. Besides the heaps of sugar, mountains of sweet balls, best of the preparation of paddy, were also given in heaps. The betels scented with camphor were also given. Thus the great sage served the king playfully with delicious food and drinks and also the beautiful garments, ornaments best of other precious material. Thus the food was served to the king.

## \|\| 24.24 \|\|

*rājovāca \|*  
*dravyāṇyetāni saciva durlabhānyaśrutāni ca \|*  
*mamāsādhyāni sahasā kvāgatānyavalokaya \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. The king on the other hand got all the articles in abundance which were beyond the reach of ordinary people. The king felt surprised while looking at the vases. The king said – О courtiers all these articles are not only beyond the reach of a man but are also unheard of. These are even beyond my reach. You find out how these have things suddenly appeared.

## \|\| 24.25 \|\|

*iti śrībrahmavaivartte mahāpurāṇe tṛtīye gaṇapatikhaṇḍe nāradanārāyaṇasaṃvāde ekadantatvapraśnaprasaṅge jadamagnikārttavīryayuddhāraṃbhavarṇanaṃnāma caturviśo'dhyāyaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. The courtier at the command of the king searched the entire cottage of the sage and told the king the astonishing news.

## \|\| 24.26 \|\|

*saciva uvāca \|*  
*dṛṣṭaṃ sarvaṃ mahārāja nibodha munimandiram \|*  
*vahnikuṇḍaṃ yajñakāṣṭhakuśapuṣpaphalānvitam \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. The courtier said – О lord, listen I have searched the entire hermitage and found the fire altars, the food for yajhas, kusa-grass, flowers, fruits, the skins of black-buck, sruvas and many of the disciples of the sage, the fire altars filled with paddy and there is no trace of wealth anywhere.

## \|\| 24.27-28 \|\|

*kṛṣṇacarmasruvasrugbhiḥ śiṣyasaṃghaiśca saṃkulam \|*  
*taijasādhārasasyādisarvasampadvivarjitam \|\| 27 \|\|*

*vṛkṣacarmaparīdhānā dṛṣṭāḥ sarve jaṭādharāḥ \|*  
*gṛhaikadeśe dṛṣṭā sā kapilaikā manoharā \|\| 28 \|\|*

27-28. All the people are wearing the bark of trees. They are ascetics with matted locks of hair on their heads. On the one side of the hermitage, there is a sacred white cow having beautiful limbs, the glory of the moon, the eyes resembling the red lotus flowers and the lustre comparable with the full moon. She appears to be burning with her own lustre and she looks like Lakshmi, the beloved of lord. She enshrines in her all the virtues and riches.

## \|\| 24.29-33 \|\|

*cārvaṃgī rudravarṇābhā raktapaṅkajalocanā \|*  
*jvalantī tejasā tatra pūrṇacandrasamaprabhā \|\| 29 \|\|*

*sarvasampadguṇādhārā sākṣādiva haripriyā \|*  
*ityevaṃ bodhito rājā durbuddhiḥ sacivājñayā \|\| 30 \|\|*

*muniṃ yayāce tāṃ dhenuṃ nibaddhaḥ kālapāśataḥ \|*  
*kiṃ vā puṇyaṃ ca kā buddhiḥ kaḥ kālaḥ sarvato balī \|\| 31 \|\|*

*puṇyavānbuddhimāndaivādrājendro 'yācata dvijam \|*  
*puṇyātprajāyate karma puṇyarūpaṃ ca bhārate \|\| 32 \|\|*

*pāpātprajāyate karma pāparūpaṃ bhayāvaham \|*  
*puṇyātkṛtvā svargabhogaṃ janma puṇyasthale nṛṇām \|\| 33 \|\|*

29-33. Thus the courtier told everything to the wicked king. Thereafter, the king under the influence of destiny demanded for the sacred cow, as the move of destiny. The meritorious and wise king influenced by the move of destiny demanded the cow from the Brahmana. In the land of Bharata, meritorious deeds emerge from merits alone and sins emerge from evil deeds. By performing good deeds a person achieves the heaven and is ultimately reborn in the sacred place of merit. Similarly the sinner has to suffer in the hell and has to be reborn as a degraded person.

## \|\| 24.34-39 \|\|

*pāpādbhuktvā ca narakaṃ kutsitaṃ janma jīvinām \|*  
*jīvināṃ niṣkṛtirnāsti sthite karmaṇi nārada \|\| 34 \|\|*

*tena kurvanti santaśca santataṃ karmaṇaḥ kṣayam \|*  
*sā vidyā tattapo jñānaṃ sa guruḥ sa ca bāndhavaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

*sā mātā sa pitā putrastatkṣayaṃ kārayettu yaḥ \|*  
*jīvināṃ dāruṇo rogaḥ karmabhogaḥ śubhāśubhaḥ \|\| 36\|\|*

*bhaktavaidyastaṃ nihanti kṛṣṇabhaktirasāyanāt \|*  
*māyā dadāti tāṃ bhaktiṃ pratijanmani sevitā \|\| 37 \|\|*

*parituṣṭā jagaddhātrī bhaktebhyo buddhidāyinī \|*  
*parā parama bhaktāya māyā yasmai dadāti ca \|\| 38 \|\|*

*māyāṃ tasmai mohayituṃ na vivekaṃ kadācana \|*  
*māyāvimohite rājā munimānīya yatnataḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

34-39. О Narada, the people can hardly have a change of rescue when they get entangled in the evil effects of their own actions. That is why the noble people always aspire for the destruction of the deeds. Because it is the same knowledge, it is the same tapas, it is the same wisdom, it is the same teacher, it is the same brother, the same parents and the same sons who really help in the destruction of the karmas. For humans it is the biggest ailment to face the result of good and bad deeds. Therefore the devotees using the medicine of adoration of Krishna, try to destroy the deeds. After serving the gods in each birth, it is the goddess Durga alone who bestows the devotion of the lord. She preserves the universe and getting pleased she bestows the noble wisdom to the devotees; she is the primeval illusion which over powers the devotees with her influence and cannot regain their senses. Therefore, the king having been influenced with the illusion went to the sage and spoke to him with folded hands, the humble words.

## \|\| 24.40 \|\|

*uvāca vinayādbhaktyā kṛtāñjalipuṭo mudā \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. The king said – О wish fulfilling tree, you give away this Kamadhenu in charity to me which fulfils all the desires. О lord of the devotees, I happen to be one of your devotees.

## \|\| 24.41-42 \|\|

*rājovāca \|*  
*dehi bhikṣāṃ kalpataro kāmadhenuṃ ca kāmadām \|*  
*mahyaṃ bhaktāya bhakteśa bhaktānugrahakāraka \|\| 41 \|\|*

*yuṣmadvidhānāṃ dātṝṇāmadeyaṃ nāsti bhārate \|*  
*dadhīcirdevatābhyaśca dadau svāsthi purā śrutam \|\| 42 \|\|*

41-42. In the land of Bharata nothing is beyond charity for a devotee like you because it is heard that the sage Dadhlci gave away his bones in the earlier times for the welfare of the gods. О best of the sages, you are the treasure of tapas and you are competent enough to create a herd of Kamadhenu cows simply with the raising of your eye-brows.

## \|\| 24.43 \|\|

*bhrūbhaṃgalīlāmātreṇa taporāśe tapodhana \|*  
*samūhaṃ kāmadhenūnāṃ sraṣṭuṃ śakto'si bhārate \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. The sage said – О king, this is just the reverse and you are talking like a wicked person or a cheat. О king, being a Brahmana, how can I give charity to a ksatriya.

## \|\| 24.44-46 \|\|

*muniruvāca \|*  
*aho vyatikramaṃ rājanbravīṣi śaṭha vañcaka \|*  
*dānaṃ dāsyāmi vipro'haṃ kṣatriyāya kathaṃ nṛpa \|\| 44 \|\|*

*kṛṣṇena dattā goloke brahmaṇe paramātmanā \|*  
*kāmadhenuriyaṃ yajñe na deyā prāṇataḥ priyā \|\| 45 \|\|*

*brahmaṇā bhṛgave dattā priyaputrāya bhūmipa \|*  
*mahyaṃ dattā ca bhṛguṇā kapilā paitṛkī mama \|\| 46 \|\|*

44-46. Lord Krishna gave away this Kamadhenu in Goloka to Brahma. Therefore it is not fit to be given away. О king, Brahma gave her to his dear son named Bhrigu and in turn the same sacred cow was given to me by Bhrigu. Therefore this cow happens to be my ancestral property. This Kamadhenu who was born in Goloka is beyond the reach of anyone in the three worlds. Therefore, how can I create such a sacred cow playfully.

## \|\| 24.47-48 \|\|

*golokajā kāmadhenurdurlabhā bhuvanatraye \|*  
*līlāmātrātkathamahaṃ kapilāṃ sraṣṭumīśvaraḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

*nāhaṃ re hāliko mūḍhastutyā notthāpito budhaḥ \|*  
*kṣaṇena bhasmasātkartuṃ kṣamo'hamatithiṃ vinā \|\| 48 \|\|*

47-48. О foolish king, I am not a farmer and the learned people never get influenced by flattery. Of course, have you not been my guest, I would have reduced you to ashes in a moment. Therefore, you get back home. I again emphasise that you get back home and don’t ignite my anger. О degraded one, misfortune has overshadowed you. You should therefore get back to your house at once and see the face of your wife and son.

## \|\| 24.49 \|\|

*gṛhaṃ gaccha gṛhaṃ gaccha me kopaṃ naiva varddhaya \|*  
*putra dārādikaṃ paśya daivabādhita pāmara \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. Hearing these words of the sage, the king at the turn of destiny, went back to his army, bowing at the feet of the sage.

## \|\| 24.50 \|\|

*munestadvacanaṃ śrutvā cukopa sa narādhipaḥ \|*  
*natvā muniṃ sainyamadhyaṃ prayayau vidhibādhitaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. Reaching there, his lips started fluttering in anger. He, therefore, deputed his army-men to forcibly take away the cow from the hermitage.

## \|\| 24.51-52 \|\|

*gatvā sainyasakāśaṃ sa kopaprasphuritādharaḥ \|*  
*kiṅkarānpreṣayāmāsa dhenumānayituṃ balāt \|\| 51 \|\|*

*kapilāsannidhiṃ gatvā ruroda munipuṃgavaḥ \|*  
*kathayāmāsa vṛttāntaṃ śokena hatacetanaḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

51-52. On the other hand Jamadagni, the sage, went to the cow and started crying before her narrating the entire story to her. Finding the Brahmana ciying, the sacred cow Surabhi who was indeed the form of Lakshmi herself said to the sage.

## \|\| 24.53-54 \|\|

*rudantaṃ brāhmaṇaṃ dṛṣṭvā surabhistamuvāca ha \|*  
*sākṣāllakṣmīsvarūpā sā bhaktānugrahakārikā \|\| 53 \|\|*

*surabhiruvāca \|*  
*indro vā hāliko vā'pi vastu svaṃ dātumīśvaraḥ \|*  
*śāstā pālayitā dātā svavastūnāṃ ca santatam \|\| 54 \|\|*

53-54. Surabhi said – Whether he is Indra or a farmer, he has the right to give away his own things. Therefore he can control, preserve or give away his own things in charity. Therefore, О sage, in case you intend to willingly hand me over to the king, then in that case, bowing at your command I shall willingly go to the king.

## \|\| 24.55 \|\|

*svecchayā cennṛpendrāya māṃ dadāsi tapodhana \|*  
*tena sārddhaṃ gamiṣyāmi svecchayā ca tavājñayā \|\| 55\|\|*

55\. But in case you are not willing to give me to the king, I shall never leave your abode. Therefore, you drive away the king with the soldiers provided by me.

## \|\| 24.56-57 \|\|

*athavā na dadāsi tvaṃ na gamiṣyāmi te gṛhāt \|*  
*matto dattena sainyena dūrīkuru nṛpaṃ dviṣam \|\| 56 \|\|*

*kathaṃ rodiṣi sarvajña māyāmohitacetanaḥ \|*  
*saṃyogaśca viyogaśca kālasādhyo na cātmanaḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

56-57. О all knowledgeable one, why are you crying? Your mind has been influenced by illusion because of the meeting with somebody and not within the competence of any individual. What are you to me and what am I to you? But your relationship with me has been established due to the time because the attachment remains till such time as the relationship is there.

## \|\| 24.58 \|\|

*tvaṃ vā ko me tavāhaṃ kā sambandhaḥ kālayojitaḥ \|*  
*yāvadeva hi sambandho mamatvaṃ tāvadeva hi \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. Whatever is known to the mind as its own and till such time as the mind accepts its ownership only up to that time he feels painful by its separation.

## \|\| 24.59 \|\|

*mano jānāti yaddravyamātmīyaṃ ceti kevalam \|*  
*duḥkhaṃ ca tasya vicchedādyāvatsvatvaṃ ca tatra vai \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. Thus speaking, the sacred cow Kamadhenu created many soldiers equipped with various types of weapons.

## \|\| 24.60-67 \|\|

*ityuktvā kāmadhenuśca suṣāva vividhāni ca \|*  
*śastrāṇyastrāṇi sainyāni sūryatulyaprabhāṇi ca \|\| 60 \|\|*

*nirgatāḥ kapilāvaktrāttrikoṭyaḥ khaḍgadhāriṇām \|*  
*vinissṛtā nāsikāyāḥ śūlinaḥ pañca koṭayaḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

*vinissṛtā locanābhyāṃ śatakoṭidhanurddharā \|*  
*kapālānnissṛtā vīrāstrikoṭyo daṇḍadhāriṇām \|\| 62 \|\|*

*vakṣassthalānnissṛtāśca trikoṭyaśśaktidhāriṇām \|*  
*śatakoṭyo gadāhastāḥ pṛṣṭhadeśādvinirgatāḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

*vinissṛtāḥ pādatalādvādyabhāṇḍāḥ sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*jaṃghādeśānnissṛtāśca trikoṭyo rājaputrakāḥ \|\| 64 \|\|*

*vinirgatā guhyadeśāttrikoṭimlecchajātayaḥ \|*  
*dattvā sainyāni kapilā munaye cābhayaṃ dadau \|*  
*yuddhaṃ kurvantu sainyāni tvaṃ na yāhītyuvāca ha \|\| 65 \|\|*

*muniḥ sambhūtasambhārairharṣayukto babhūva ha \|*  
*nṛpeṇa prerito bhṛtyo nṛpaṃ sarvamuvāca ha \|\| 66 \|\|*

*kapilāsainyavṛttāntamātmavargaparājayam \|*  
*tacchrutvā nṛpaśārdūlastrastaḥ kātaramānasaḥ \|*  
*dūtāntsaṃpreṣya sainyāni cājahāra svadeśataḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

60-67. Thereafter, three crores of soldiers appeared from the mouth of Kapila the cow, who were holding swords and shields, five crores of soldiers holding tridents, a hundred crores of soldiers from the eyes holding bow and arrow, three crores of soldiers, carrying staff and three crores of warriors holding saktis and a hundred crores of warriors holding clubs. Thousands of drummers emerged from the soles of hoofs, besides three crores of Rajaputras from the thighs and from private parts and three crores of soldiers who were Mlecchas. Thus the sacred cow Kapila handed over the entire army of soldiers to the sage and granted him full protection. She said these soldiers will go immediately and fight with the king’s army. You do not have to go there. Thus getting protected with so great an army, the sage was delighted. The messengers of the king went back to him and conveyed the news of the army of Kapila. On realising the possibility of his defeat, the king felt panicky and deputing his messengers he called for more army from his kingdom.

## Chapter 25

## \|\| 25.1 \|\|

*nārāyaṇa uvāca \|*  
*hariṃ smarankārtavīryyo hṛdayena vidūyatā \|*  
*dūtaṃ prasthāpayāmāsa kupito munisannidhim \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Narayana said – The king Kartavirya was painful at heart and in anger he sent a messenger to the sage.

## \|\| 25.2 \|\|

*yuddhaṃ dehi muniśreṣṭha kiṃ vā dhenuṃ ca vāñchitām \|*  
*mahyaṃ bhṛtyāyātithaye suvicāryya yathocitam \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. He said, "O best of the sage, I am your servant and a guest, therefore you give me appropriately either the sacred cow or the battle, whatever you deem better."

## \|\| 25.3 \|\|

*dūtasya vacanaṃ śrutvā jahāsa munipuṃgavaḥ \|*  
*hitaṃ satyaṃ nītisāraṃ sarvaṃ dūtamuvāca ha \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. On hearing the words of the messengers, the sage laughed and spoke to the messenger, the words which were beneficial, truthful and quite appropriate.

## \|\| 25.4 \|\|

*muniruvāca \|*  
*dṛṣṭo nṛpo nirāhāraḥ samānīto mayā gṛham \|*  
*vividhaṃ ca yathāśaktyā bhojitaśca yathocitam \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. The sage said – Finding the king hungry I brought him to my abode and served him with food appropriately whatever was available with me.

## \|\| 25.5 \|\|

*kapilāṃ yācate rājā mama prāṇādhikāṃ priyām \|*  
*tāṃ dātumakṣamo dūta yuddhaṃ dāsyāmi niścitam \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. О messengers, now the same king is demanding my sacred cow from me which is dearer to me than my life. I am therefore unable to part with her. I will fight now; this is my resolve.

## \|\| 25.6 \|\|

*munestadvacanaṃ śrutvā dūtaḥ sarvamuvāca ha \|*  
*nṛpendraṃ ca sabhāmadhye sannāhaissaṃyutaṃ bhiyā \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. On hearing the words of the sage, the messenger felt panicky at heart and spoke to the king whatever the sage had told him.

## \|\| 25.7 \|\|

*muniśca kapilāmāha sāmprataṃ kiṅkaromyaham \|*  
*karṇadhāraṃ vinā naukā tathā sainyaṃ vinā mayā \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Thereafter, the sage spoke to the cow. "You tell me what I should do now, because the army is like a boat without a sailor."

## \|\| 25.8 \|\|

*kapilā ca dadau tasmai śastrāṇi vividhāni ca \|*  
*yuddhaśāstropadeśaṃ ca sandhānaṃ caupayogikam \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Kapila then imparted the sage with the knowledge of the use of various types of weapons and arrows and other tricks of the battlefield.

## \|\| 25.9-11 \|\|

*jayaṃ bhavatu te vipra yuddhe jeṣyasi niścitam \|*  
*tava mṛtyurna bhavitā satyamastraṃ vinā mune \|\| 9 \|\|*

*nṛpeṇa sārddhaṃ te yuddhamayuktaṃ brāhmaṇasya ca \|*  
*dattātreyasya śiṣyeṇa vyarthaṃ vai śaktidhāriṇā \|*  
*ityuktvā kapilā brahmanvirarāma manasvinī \|\| 10 \|\|*

*munirmanasvī sainyaṃ ca sajjīkṛtya tato mune \|*  
*gṛhītvā sarvasainyaṃ ca sa jagāma raṇājiram \|\| 11 \|\|*

9-11. She then said, "О Brahmana, you will surely be victorious in the battle-field." О sage, you are not supposed to die without the Satyastra. A Brahmana is going to fight with a king who is the disciple of Dattatreya and is quite powerful, is quite valorous but useless. Thus speaking, О Brahmana, the sacred cow kept quiet. Thereafter the sage kept the army ready and proceeded on to the battle-field.

## \|\| 25.12 \|\|

*rājā jagāma yuddhāya nanāma munipuṃgavam \|*  
*ubhayoḥ sainyayoryuddhaṃ babhūva bahuduṣkaram \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The king also bowed in reverence to the sage Jamadagni reaching in the battle-field. Thereafter the battle between the two armies started.

## \|\| 25.13-15 \|\|

*rājasainyaṃ jitaṃ sarvaṃ kapilāsenayā balāt \|*  
*vicitraṃ ca rathaṃ rājño babhañje līlayā raṇe \|\| 13 \|\|*

*dhanuściccheda sajyaṃ tatsā senā kāpilī mudā \|*  
*nṛpendraḥ kāpileyāni jetuṃ sainyāni cākṣamaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

*sainyāni taṃ śastravṛṣṭyā nyastaśastraṃ cakāra ha \|*  
*śaravṛṣṭyā śastravṛṣṭyā rājā mūrcchāmavāpa ha \|\| 15\|\|*

13-15. The soldiers of Kapila forcefully defeated the army of the king and the chariot of the king was also playfully broken. His kavacha was cut-off; the king was unable to be victorious over the army of Kapila. By the showering of the weapons, the army was deprived of the arrows and the weapons.

## \|\| 25.16-17 \|\|

*kiñcicchiṣṭaṃ balaṃ rājñaḥ kiṃcideva palāyitam \|*  
*munīndro mūrcchitaṃ dṛṣṭvā nṛpendramatithiṃ mune \|\| 16 \|\|*

*kṛpānidhiśca kṛpayā tatsainyaṃ sañjahāra ca \|*  
*gatvā sainyaṃ vilīnaṃ ca kapilāyāṃ ca kṛtrimam \|\| 17 \|\|*

16-17. O sage, only a small number of the king’s army could escape and some of the soldiers fled from the battle field. The merciful sage found the king fainted.

## \|\| 25.18 \|\|

*nṛpāya muninā śīghraṃ dattāścaraṇareṇavaḥ \|*  
*āśīrvādaṃ pradattaṃ ca jayo'stviti kṛpālunā \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Thereafter, the sage gave the king the dust of his feet besides the blessings, saying, "You will be victorious." Thus speaking he took away the water from the Kamandalu and brought him back to life.

## \|\| 25.19-21 \|\|

*kamaṇḍalujalaṃ prokṣya jīvayāmāsa taṃ nṛpam \|*  
*sa rājā cetanāṃ prāpya samutthāya raṇājirāt \|\| 19 \|\|*

*mūrdhnā nanāma bhaktyā ca muniśreṣṭhaṃ kṛtāñjaliḥ \|*  
*muniḥ śubhāśiṣaṃ dattvā rājānaṃ tvāliliṅga saḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

*punastaṃ snāpayitvā ca bhojayāmāsa yatnataḥ \|*  
*navanītaṃ hi hṛdayaṃ brāhmaṇānāṃ tu santatam \|\| 21 \|\|*

19-21. On regaining consciousness, the king came out of the battle-field and spoke humble words, folding his hands to the sage bowing in reverence to him at the same time. The sage blessed him variously and embraced the king. The king was then bathed and was served food. The mind of the Brahmanas is always soft like butter.

## \|\| 25.22 \|\|

*anyeṣāṃ kṣuradhārābhamasādhyaṃ dāruṇaṃ sadā \|*  
*uvāca taṃ muniśreṣṭho gṛhaṃ gaccha dharādhipa \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. The mind of other people is always sharp like the edge of a blade, which is quite stiff and hard. Thereafter the sage said, "O king you go back to your abode."

## \|\| 25.23 \|\|

*rājovāca \|*  
*raṇaṃ dehi mahābāho dhenuṃ kiṃ vā mayepsitām \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. The king said – "O great warrior I shall not return home; you kindly give me the battle or the cow desired by me."

## Chapter 26

## \|\| 26.1 \|\|

*nārāyaṇa uvāca \|*  
*hariṃ smaranmuniśreṣṭho vākyaṃ śrutvā ca bhūbhṛtaḥ \|*  
*hitaṃ satyaṃ nītisāraṃ pravaktusupacakrame \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Narayana said – "On hearing the words of the king, the best of the sages started speaking to him, reminding himself of the name of the lord, which were quite beneficial, truthful and the full of essence."

## \|\| 26.2 \|\|

*muniruvāca \|*  
*gṛhaṃ gaccha mahābhāga rakṣa dharmaṃ sanātanam \|*  
*sarvasampatsthirā śaśvatsthite dharme suniścitam \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. The sage said – "O virtuous one, you go back to your house and protect your everlasting dharma because by falling from the dharma, the wisdom remains stable." This is certain.

## \|\| 26.3-4 \|\|

*tvāṃ ca dṛṣṭvā nirāhāraṃ samānīya gṛhaṃ nṛpa \|*  
*tava pūjāmakaravaṃ yathāśakti vidhānataḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

*sāmprataṃ mūrchitaṃ dṛṣṭvā pādareṇuṃ śubhāśiṣam \|*  
*adadāṃ cetayāñcakre vaktumevocitaṃ na ca \|\| 4 \|\|*

3-4. О king, finding you hungry I brought to you my abode and extended you all the honour appropriately. Currently also finding you fainted I blessed you pouring the dust of my feet over you, which brought you back to your senses. This is not appropriate to be talked."

## \|\| 26.5-7 \|\|

*nṛpastadvacanaṃ śrutvā praṇamya munipuṃgavam \|*  
*rathamanyaṃ tvāruroha yuddhaṃ dehītyuvāca ha \|\| 5 \|\|*

*muniḥ kṛtvā ca sannāhaṃ taṃ yoddhumupacakrame \|*  
*rājā taṃ yuyudhe tatra kopenāhatacetanaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

*kapilādattaśastreṇa nyastaśastraṃ cakāra tam \|*  
*kapilādattayā śaktyā punarmūrcchāmavāpa ca \|\| 7 \|\|*

5-7. On hearing the words of the sage the king bowed in reverence to the sage and mounting on the chariot, said, "You allow me to fight." Thereafter wearing the kavacha, the sage started fighting with him. The king on his part getting enraged fought a fierce battle. The sage on the other hand with the use of these weapons by the sacred cow, relieved the king of all the weapons. Thereafter, with the use of sakti provided by Kapila, the king again fainted.

## \|\| 26.8-10 \|\|

*punaśca cetanāṃ prāpya rājā rājīvalocanaḥ \|*  
*muninā yuyudhe tatra kopena punareva ca \|\| 8 \|\|*

*āgneyaṃ yojayāmāsa samare nṛpapuṃgavaḥ \|*  
*munirnirvāpayāmāsa vāruṇena ca līlayā \|\| 9 \|\|*

*nṛpendro vāruṇāstraṃ ca cikṣepa samare munau \|*  
*vāyavyāstreṇa sa muniḥ śamayāmāsa līlayā \|\| 10 \|\|*

8-10. Thereafter regaining consciousness, the king having the lotus-like eyes, fought a fierce battle with the sage; then the best of the king used the fire-arrow on the sage in the battle-field which was neutralised by the sage with the use of Varunastra. The king on his part used in the battle-field the Varunastra quite playfully.

## \|\| 26.11-12 \|\|

*vāyavyāstraṃ nṛpaśreṣṭhaścikṣepa samare tadā \|*  
*gāndharveṇa muniśreṣṭhaḥ śamayāmāsa tatkṣaṇam \|\| 11 \|\|*

*nāgāstraṃ ca nṛpaśreṣṭhaścikṣepa raṇamūrddhani \|*  
*gāruḍena muniśreṣṭho nijaghāna kṣaṇānmudā \|\| 12 \|\|*

11-12. The king attacked the sage with Vayavyastra and the sage neutralised it with the use of Gandharvastra. The king used serpent-arrow on the sage in the battle-field and the sage in turn, in an instant, shot, a Garuda arrow destroying the weapons of the king.

## \|\| 26.13-14 \|\|

*māheśvaraṃ mahāstraṃ ca śatasūryyasamaprabham \|*  
*cikṣepa nṛpatiśreṣṭho dyotayantaṃ diśo daśa \|\| 13 \|\|*

*vaiṣṇavāstreṇa divyena trilokavyāpakena ca \|*  
*munirnirvāpayāmāsa bahuyatnena nārada \|\| 14 \|\|*

13-14. O Narada, the king then shot Maheshvarastra on the sage which was the terrific of all and emitted lustre like hundreds of suns illumining the entire universe. The sage on the other hand, neutralised the same with the use of divine Vais navastra which pervaded the three worlds.

## \|\| 26.15-16 \|\|

*munirnārāyaṇāstraṃ ca cikṣipe mantrapūrvakam \|*  
*śastraṃ dṛṣṭvā mahārājo nanāma śaraṇaṃ yayau \|\| 15 \|\|*

*ūrdhvaṃ ca bhramaṇaṃ kṛtvā kṣaṇaṃ dīptvā diśo daśa \|*  
*pralayāgnisamaṃ tatra svayamantaradhīyata \|\| 16 \|\|*

15-16. Thereafter, the sage attacked the king with Narayanastra reciting the mantra. The king throwing away all the weapons, bowed in reverence to the same and took refuge under it. The weapon started roaming in all the directions in the universe emitting flames of fire like the fire of dissolution out of it.

## \|\| 26.17 \|\|

*jṛmbhaṇāstraṃ ca sa muniścikṣepa raṇamūrddhani \|*  
*nidrāṃ prāpattena rājā suṣvāpa ca mṛto yathā \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. The sage then used in the battle-field Jrmbhanastra as a result of which the king felt sleepy and he went to sleep as if dead.

## \|\| 26.18 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā nṛpaṃ nidritaṃ taṃ cārddhacandreṇa tatkṣaṇam \|*  
*ciccheda sārathiṃ yānaṃ dhanurbāṇaṃ munistadā \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Finding that the king was so sleeping, the sage used the crescent arrow which destroys the king’s chariot, the charioteer, the bow and the arrows.

## \|\| 26.19 \|\|

*mukuṭaṃ ca kṣurapreṇa cchatraṃ sannāhameva ca \|*  
*astraṃ tṛṇaṃ vājigaṇaṃ vividhe na ca bhūbhṛtaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. As a result of the shooting of the arrow, the crown of the king, the chatra, the kavacha the weapons, the quiver and the horses also were cut-off.

## \|\| 26.20-21 \|\|

*munistatsacivānsarvānnāgāstreṇaiva līlayā \|*  
*nibadhya sthāpayāmāsa prahasya samarasthale \|\| 20 \|\|*

*munistaṃ bodhayāmāsa sumantreṇaiva līlayā \|*  
*nibaddhasarvāmātyānāṃ darśayāmāsa bhūmipam \|\| 21 \|\|*

20-21. Thereafter the sage with the use of Nagdstra captured the king as well as his courtiers. Thereafter Sumantra brought back to the king to sense and showed him the captured courtiers.

## \|\| 26.22 \|\|

*darśayitvā nṛpaṃ tāṃśca mocayāmāsa tatkṣaṇam \|*  
*nṛpendramāśiṣaṃ kṛtvā gṛhaṃ gacchetyuvāca ha \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Thereafter showing the same to the king he was freed. Blessing him, the sage said, "You go back home."

## \|\| 26.23 \|\|

*rājā kopātsamutthāya śūlamudyamya yatnataḥ \|*  
*cikṣepa taṃ muniśreṣṭhaṃ muniḥ śaktyā jaghāna tam \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Getting enraged the king got up and attacked the sage with a trident which was destroyed by the sage with his shakti.

## \|\| 26.24 \|\|

*etasminnantare brahmā samāgatya raṇasthalam \|*  
*sa prītiṃ janayāmāsa sunītyā ca parasparam \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. In the meantime Brahma arrived there and extending nice advise to both of them, he developed love for each other in the mind of both of them.

## \|\| 26.25-26 \|\|

*munirnanāma brahmāṇaṃ santuṣṭaśca raṇasthale \|*  
*rājā natvā vidhiṃ carṣiṃ svapuraṃ prayayau tadā \|\| 25 \|\|*

*iti śrībrahmavaivartte mahāpurāṇe tṛtīye gaṇapatikhaṇḍe nāradanārāyaṇa saṃvāde jamadagnikārtavīryayuddhopaśamavarṇanaṃ nāma ṣaḍviṃśo'dhyāyaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

25-26. Getting satisfied in the battle-field, the sage bowed before the king. The king on the other hand bowed in reverence to Brahma as well as the sage and went back to his kingdom while the sage reverted to his abode. Brahma on the other hand also went back to his abode. I have spoken all this to you, what more do you want to listen to from me?

## Chapter 27

## \|\| 27.1 \|\|

*nārāyaṇa uvāca \|*  
*hariṃ smṛtvā gṛhaṃ gatvā rājā vismitamānasaḥ \|*  
*ājagāma mahāraṇye jamadagnyāśramaṃ punaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Narayana said – The king reciting the name of the lord and getting surprised left for his abode. He once again arrived at the hermitage of Jamadagni.

## \|\| 27.2 \|\|

*rathānāṃ ca caturlakṣaṃ rathināṃ daśalakṣakam \|*  
*aśvendrāṇāṃ gajendrāṇāṃ padā tīnāmasaṃkhyakam \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. He was accompanied by four lakhs of chariots, ten lakhs of soldiers mounted on chariots, one lakh big horses besides elephants, foot soldiers, the number of which could not be estimated.

## \|\| 27.3-4 \|\|

*rājendrāṇāṃ sahasraṃ ca mahābalaparākramam \|*  
*mahāsamṛddhiyuktaśca trailokyaṃ jetumīśvaraḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

*sarvato veṣṭayāmāsa jamadagnyāśramaṃ mudā \|*  
*rathastho varmayuktaśca kārttavīryyārjunaḥ svayam \|\| 4 \|\|*

3-4. There were a lakh of others kings who were great, valorous and strong. Thus the king arrived there with a well equipped army and could conquer the three worlds. He surrounded the hermitage of Jamadagni from all sides. Kartaviryarjuna was mounted on a chariot wearing the kavacha.

## \|\| 27.5 \|\|

*sainyaśabdairvādyaśabdairmahākolāhalairmune \|*  
*jamadagnyāśramasthāśca mūrcchāmāpurbhayena ca \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. О sage, with the sound produced from the playing of musical instruments and with the terrific sound of the movement of the troops all the people in the hermitage of Jamadagni fainted.

## \|\| 27.6 \|\|

*kuṭīṃ praviśya balavāngṛhītvā kapilāṃ śubhām \|*  
*puraṃ gantuṃ manaścakre durbuddhirasadāśrayaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. The valorous king entered the hermitage and caught hold of the Kapila cow and the degraded king dragged her and thought of carrying her to his abode.

## \|\| 27.7-8 \|\|

*samuttasthau muniśreṣṭho gṛhītvā saśaraṃ dhanuḥ \|*  
*ekākī muktagātraśca dhenuṃ natvā hariṃ smaran \|\| 7 \|\|*

*āśramasthāñjanānsarvānsamāśvāsya ca yatnataḥ \|*  
*ājagāma raṇasthānaṃ niśśaṅko nṛpateḥ puraḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

7-8. Thereafter, the dwellers of the hermitage of the sage took the bows and arrows in their hands, with naked bodies bowing at the feet of the cow, reciting the name of the lord, assuring variously, appeared before the king for a fight.

## \|\| 27.9 \|\|

*nirmame śarajālaṃ ca sa munirmantrapūrvakam \|*  
*ācchādayatsvāśramaṃ tairmānavaṃ varmaṇā yathā \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. The sage making great effort created a net of arrows and he covered his entire hermitage with the net of arrows as if a human being was wearing a kavacha.

## \|\| 27.10 \|\|

*aparaṃ śarajālaṃ ca nirmame munipuṅgavaḥ \|*  
*taireva vārayāmāsa sarvasainyaṃ yathākramam \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. The sage then created another net of arrows and surrounded the entire army of the king with the same.

## \|\| 27.11 \|\|

*muninā śarajālena sarvasainyaṃ samāvṛtam \|*  
*tāni sarvāṇi guptāni yathā patrāṇi pañjare \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Thus with the net of the arrows created by the sage, the entire royal army was imprisoned in it like a bird placed in a cage.

## \|\| 27.12 \|\|

*rājā dṛṣṭvā muniśreṣṭhamavaruhya rathātpuraḥ \|*  
*sārddhaṃ nṛpendrairbhaktyā ca praṇanāma kṛtājjaliḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Thereafter finding the army thus imprisoned there, the king got down from his chariot and with folded hands together with all other kings bowed in reverence to the sage.

## \|\| 27.13-16 \|\|

*natvā''ruroha yānaṃ sa muneḥ prāpya śubhāśiṣaḥ \|*  
*āruhya ca nṛpaśreṣṭhaḥ svayānaṃ hṛṣṭamānasaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

*nṛpaiḥ sārddhaṃ nṛpaśreṣṭhaścikṣepa munipuṅgave \|*  
*astraṃ śastraṃ gadāṃ śaktiṃ jaghāna krīḍayā muniḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

*muniścikṣepa divyāstraṃ cicchide līlayā nṛpaḥ \|*  
*śūlaṃ cikṣepa nṛpatistañjaghāna tadā muniḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

*aparaṃ śarajālaṃ ca nirmame munipuṅgavaḥ \|*  
*śastraughairdurnivāryyaiśca khaṇḍaṃ khaṇḍaṃ cakāra ca saḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

13-16. The sage blessed them variously; thereafter the king was delighted and mounted his chariot. The king then together with all other kings started shooting arrows, clubs and saktis but the sage destroyed all of them playfully. The sage also used his own weapons which were also shattered by the king who ultimately used the trident to attack the sage. The trident was destroyed by the sage and he created another net of arrows but the king with the use of his terrific weapons shattered the same.

## \|\| 27.17-19 \|\|

*nibaddhāśśarajālena na ca śaktāḥ palāyitum \|*  
*jṛmbhaṇāstreṇa muninā te ca sarve vijṛmbhitāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

*hastyaśvarathapādātasahitaṃ sarvasainyakam \|*  
*rājānaṃ nidritaṃ dṛṣṭvā na jaghāna munīśvaraḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

*gṛhītvā kapilāṃ hṛṣṭo rudantīṃ śokamūrcchitām \|*  
*bodhayitvā puraḥ kṛtvā svāśramaṃ gantumudyataḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

17-19. Those who were captured in the net of arrows, could not -escape anywhere. Thereafter, the sage with the use of Jrmbhanastra, made all the elephants, the horses, the foot soldiers and all other soldiers to be overpowered in a deep swoon. Finding the king asleep, the sage, did not kill him.

## \|\| 27.20 \|\|

*etasminnantare rājā cetanāṃ prāpya nārada \|*  
*nivārayāmāsa muniṃ gṛhītvā saśaraṃ dhanuḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Delightfully he brought to senses the Kapila cow who had fainted while weeping and taking her with him he returned to his Asrama.

## \|\| 27.21 \|\|

*jagāma kapilā trastā svasthānaṃ ca raṇājirāt \|*  
*muniśca tasthau niśśaṅko gṛhītvā saśaraṃ dhanuḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. In the meantime, the king recovered his senses, prevented the movement of the sage and the cow with the use of his arrows.

## \|\| 27.22 \|\|

*brahmāstraṃ ca nṛpaśreṣṭhaḥ sa cikṣepa munau tadā \|*  
*brahmāstreṇa munīndrasya sadyo nirvāṇatāṃ gatam \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. But inspite of the danger ahead he left the battle-field and went to his abode. The sage thereafter moved fearlessly towards his hermitage.

## \|\| 27.23-24 \|\|

*divyāstreṇa muniśreṣṭho nṛpasya saśaraṃ dhanuḥ \|*  
*rathaṃ ca sārathiṃ caiva cicchide varma durvaham \|\| 23 \|\|*

*atha rājā mahākruddho dadarśa svasamīpataḥ \|*  
*dattena dattāṃ śaktiṃ tāmekapūruṣaghātinīm \|\| 24 \|\|*

23-24. The king then shot Brahmdstra on the sage which was neutralised by the sage with the use of his own Brahmastra. The sage then with the use of divine weapons with him, neutralised all the weapons of the king including his bows and arrows, the chariot with charioteer and the horrible kavacha also.

## \|\| 27.25 \|\|

*jagrāha natvā dattaṃ taṃ sa natvā śaktimulbaṇām \|*  
*cūrṇayāmāsa tatraiva śatasūryyasamaprabhām \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. This enraged the king who looked at the sakti which was lying close by and could surely destroy a human being and was bestowed by Dattatreya.

## \|\| 27.26 \|\|

*yattejaḥ sarvadevānāṃ tejo nārāyaṇasya ca \|*  
*śambhośca brahmaṇaścaiva māyāyāścaiva nārada \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. The king mentally bowed in reverence to Dattatreya and then to the sakti. Thereafter the sakti which dazzled like hundreds of suns and started wielding the same in the battle-field.

## \|\| 27.27-28 \|\|

*tatraivāvāhayāmāsa sa yogī mantrapūrvakam \|*  
*tejasā dyotayāmāsa gaganaṃ ca diśo daśa \|\| 27 \|\|*

*iti śrībrahmavaivartte mahāpurāṇe tṛtīye gaṇapatikhaṇḍe nāradanārāyaṇasaṃvāde jamadagnisaṃhāraparaśurāmapratijñādivarṇanaṃnāma saptaviṃśo'dhyāyaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

27-28. O Narada, the king invoked the lustre of all the gods, Narayana, Shiva, Brahma and the illusion reciting the mantras as a result of which, the lustre of the same was spread in all the ten directions.

## \|\| 27.29 \|\|

*cikṣepa tāṃ cūrṇayitvā kārtavīryyārjunaḥ svayam \|*  
*sadyaḥ papāta sā śaktirjvalantī munivakṣasi \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Finding the king using that sakti on the sage, all the gods who had assembled there to witness the battle started crying in terror.

## \|\| 27.30 \|\|

*vidāryyoro muneḥ śaktirjagāma harisannidhim \|*  
*dattāya hariṇā dattā śastrāstranidhaye tadā \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Kartaviryarjuna had himself shot it wielding in the air. The sakti got ignited and struck at the chest of the sage.

## \|\| 27.31 \|\|

*mūrcchāṃ samprāpya sa muniḥ prāṇāṃstatyāja tatkṣaṇam \|*  
*tejo'mbare bhramitvā ca brahmalokaṃ jagāma ha \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. It pierced through the heart of the sage and then went to the lord which was given by the lord to Dattatreya.

## \|\| 27.32 \|\|

*yuddhe muniṃ mṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā ruroda kapilā muhuḥ \|*  
*he tāta tātetyuccāryya golokaṃ sā jagāma ha \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. The sage fainted instantaneously and the life departed out of his body. His lustre went to Brahmaloka while roaming in the air for some time.

## \|\| 27.33 \|\|

*sarvaṃ sā kathayāmāsa goloke kṛṣṇamīśvaram \|*  
*ratnasiṃhāsanasthaṃ taṃ gopairgopībhirāvṛtam \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Finding the sage killed in the battle-field, the Kapila cow started crying in the battle-field. She said, "O lord" and thus speaking she went to the Goloka.

## \|\| 27.34 \|\|

*kṛṣṇena brahmaṇe dattā brahmaṇā bhṛgave purā \|*  
*sā prītyā puṣkare brahmanbhṛguṇā jamadagnaye \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Reaching Goloka she narrated the entire story to lord Krishna who was seated there, on the gemstudded lion-throne comfortably and was surrounded by the cow-herds and cowherdesses.

## \|\| 27.35 \|\|

*natvā ca kāmadhenūnāṃ samūhaṃ sā jagāma ha \|*  
*tadaśrubindunā marttye ratnasaṃdho babhūva ha \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. О Brahmana, the cow was first of all given by lord Krishna to Brahma. Brahma gave her to Bhrigu and Bhrigu lovingly gave her away to Jamadagni at Puskara.

## \|\| 27.36 \|\|

*atha rājā taṃ nihatya bodhayitvā svasainyakam \|*  
*prāyaścittaṃ vinirvartya jagāma svapuraṃ mudā \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Thereafter, she bowing in reverence to the group of Kamadhenu cows left the place. With the tears dropping from her eyes pearls were formed on earth.

## \|\| 27.37 \|\|

*prāṇanāthaṃ mṛtaṃ śrutvā jagāma reṇukā satī \|*  
*muniṃ vakṣasi saṃsthāpya kṣaṇaṃ mūrcchā mavāpa sā \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. The king after killing Jamadagni performed repentance with his soldiers and left for his place.

## \|\| 27.38 \|\|

*tataḥ sā cetanāṃ prāpya na ruroda pativratā \|*  
*ehi vatsa bhṛgo rāma rāma rāmetyuvāca ha \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Finding his lord having been killed, the chaste Menaka arrived there taking the body in her lap, she fainted.

## \|\| 27.39 \|\|

*ājagāma bhṛgustūrṇaṃ kṣaṇādvai puṣkarādaho \|*  
*nanāma mātaraṃ bhaktyā manoyāyī ca yogavit \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. On regaining consciousness the chaste lady, did not cry but reciting the name of Rama and her husband started summoning Parashurama.

## \|\| 27.40 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā rāmo mṛtaṃ tātaṃ śokārtāṃ jananīṃ satīm \|*  
*ākarṇya raṇavṛttāntaṃ prayāntīṃ kapilāṃ śucā \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Parashurama on the other hand who could move at the speed thought and was well-versed in the yogas arrived there from Puskara and he bowed in reverence to his mother.

## \|\| 27.41-42 \|\|

*vilalāpa bhṛśaṃ tatra he tāta jananīti ca \|*  
*citāṃ cakāra yogī ndraścandanairājyasaṃyutām \|\| 41 \|\|*

*reṇukā rāmamādāya tūrṇaṃ kṛtvā svavakṣasi \|*  
*cucumba gaṇḍe śirasi rurodoccairbhṛśaṃ muhuḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

41-42. Parashurama then found his father having been killed, the mother filled with grief and the cow Kapila having proceeded to Goloka. He also learnt about the news of the battle. He lamented again and again reciting the name of his father and mother. Thereafter he, who was the best of the yogis prepared a pyre of a sandal-wood.

## \|\| 27.43 \|\|

*rāma rāma mahābāho kva yāmi tvāṃ vihāya ca \|*  
*vatsa vatseti kṛtvaivaṃ vilalāpa bhṛśaṃ muhuḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Renuka on the other hand embraced Parashurama, kissed his cheeks and head. Thereafter she started crying aloud.

## \|\| 27.44 \|\|

*matprāṇādhika he vatsa madīyaṃ vacanaṃ śṛṇu \|*  
*pitroḥ śeṣakriyāṃ kṛtvā yāyā yuddhaṃ na putraka \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. She uttered, "O Rama, where should I proceed leaving you." Thus speaking she went on lamenting again and again.

## \|\| 27.45-46 \|\|

*gṛhe tiṣṭha sukhaṃ vatsa tapasyāṃ kuru śāśvatīm \|*  
*samaraṃ naiva sukhadaṃ dāruṇaiḥ kṣatriyaiḥ saha \|\| 45 \|\|*

*māturvacanamaśrutvā pratijñāṃ tāṃ cakāra ha \|*  
*trissaptakṛtvo nirbhūpāṃ kariṣyāmi dhruvaṃ mahīm \|\| 46\|\|*

45-46. О son, you have been dearer to me than my life even. On son, you don’t go to the battle-field after performing the last rites of your parents. О son, you remain in your abode comfortably and perform tapas but don’t fight with terrific ks atrlyas because by doing so, the result becomes unpleasant."

## \|\| 27.47-49 \|\|

*kārtavīryaṃ haniṣyāmi līlayā kṣatriyādhamam \|*  
*pitṝṃśca tarpayiṣyāmi kṣatriyakṣatajaistathā \|\| 47 \|\|*

*ityudīryya puro māturvilalāpa muhurmuhuḥ \|*  
*hitaṃ tathyaṃ nītisāraṃ bodhayāmāsa mātaram \|\| 48 \|\|*

*rāma uvāca \|*  
*pituḥ śāsanahantāraṃ piturvadhavidhāyakam \|*  
*yo na hanti mahāmūḍho rauravaṃ sa vrajed dhruvam \|\| 49 \|\|*

47-49. Thus hearing the words of his mother, Parashurama took a vow that "I shall surely rid the earth of the ksatrlyas twenty one times and I shall kill this degraded Kartavirya playfully." I shall then offer tarpana to my ancestors with the blood of Kartavirya." Thus taking a vow before his mother, Paras'urama again started lamenting. Thereafter he started speaking to his mother, the words which were prosperous, truthful and according to the scriptures.

## \|\| 27.50 \|\|

*agnido garadaścaiva śastrapāṇirdhanāpahaḥ \|*  
*kṣetradārāpahārī ca pitṛbandhuvihiṃsakaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. Parashurama said – The one who disobeys the command of his father and does not kill the killer of the father, such a foolish fellow surely falls in the terrific hell.

## \|\| 27.51 \|\|

*satataṃ mandakārī ca nindakaḥ kaṭujalpakaḥ \|*  
*ekādaśaite pāpiṣṭhā vadhārhā vedasammatāḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. The one who ignites fire, administers poison or holds weapons or the one who snatches away the riches and the one who snatches away another fields and the wife, the one who kills the father and the brother, the one who is always lazy, the back-biter, the one who always speaks harsh words, such people are always treated to be sinners and according to the provision of the Vedas, they are fit to be killed.

## \|\| 27.52 \|\|

*dvijānāṃ draviṇādānaṃ sthānānnirvāsanaṃ sati \|*  
*vapanaṃ tāḍanaṃ caiva vadhamāhurmanīṣiṇaḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. The one who snatches away the riches, drives away others' possessions, the shaving of the head or denouncement these are considered to be the death for the Brahmana.

## \|\| 27.53-54 \|\|

*etasminnantare tatra cājagāma bhṛguḥ svayam \|*  
*atitrasto manasvī ca hṛdayena vidūyatā \|\| 53 \|\|*

*dṛṣṭvā taṃ reṇukārāmau vinatau saṃbabhūvatuḥ \|*  
*sa tāvuvāca vedoktaṃ paralokahitāya ca \|\| 54\|\|*

53-54. In the meantime the sage Bhrigu himself arrived there and he started expressing his heart felt grief. Finding both Parashurama and Renuka in a melancholy mood he started speaking to them the words which was according to the Vedas and were beneficial even for the next world.

## \|\| 27.55 \|\|

*bhṛguruvāca \|*  
*madvaṃśajāto jñānī tvaṃ kathaṃ vilapase suta \|*  
*jalabudbudavatsarvaṃ saṃsāre ca carācaram \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. Bhrigu said – О son, you are born in my race and are very well-read. Why are you lamenting? Because everything in the universe is perishable like the bubble of water.

## \|\| 27.56 \|\|

*satyasāraṃ satyabījaṃ kṛṣṇaṃ cintaya putraka \|*  
*yad gataṃ tadgataṃ vatsa gataṃ naivāgamiṣyati \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. О son, recite the name of lord Krishna, who is the essence of tmth and is quite truthful as well. О son, whosoever is gone is gone. The one who has gone can never come back.

## \|\| 27.57 \|\|

*yadbhavettadbhavatyeva bhavitā yadbhaviṣyati \|*  
*pūrvārjitaṃ svīyakarma phalaṃ kena nivāryyate \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. Whatever is destined to happen, it happens always. Who can prevent one from facing the results of the deeds performed by him in earlier births?

## \|\| 27.58-59 \|\|

*bhūtaṃ bhavyaṃ bhaviṣyaṃ ca yatkṛṣṇena nirūpitam \|*  
*nirūpitaṃ yattatkarma kena vatsa nivāryate \|\| 58 \|\|*

*māyābījaṃ māyināṃ ca śarīraṃ pāñcabhautikam \|*  
*saṃketapūrvakaṃ nāma prātaḥ svapnasamaṃ suta \|\| 59 \|\|*

58-59. O son, whatever past, present and future is there, has been built preordained by lord Krishna on the basis of the deeds which are destined to be performed; who can prevent them? О son this body of five elements is the seed of illusion which vanishes like the dream of the morning hours.

## \|\| 27.60 \|\|

*kṣudhā nidrā dayā śāntiḥ kṣamā kāntyādayastathā \|*  
*yānti prāṇā mano jñānaṃ prayāte paramātmani \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. With the departing of the soul from the body, hunger, sleep, mercy, glory, forgiveness and grace besides the wisdom of the mind also depart from the body.

## \|\| 27.61 \|\|

*buddhiśca śaktayaḥ sarvā rājendramiva kiṃkarāḥ \|*  
*sarve tamanugacchanti taṃ kṛṣṇaṃ bhaja yatnataḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. His wisdom and all his strength also leave the body like the servants of the king who always follow him. Therefore, you make an effort to recite the name of lord Krishna,

## \|\| 27.62 \|\|

*ke vā keṣāṃ ca pitaraḥ ke vā keṣāṃ sutāḥ suta \|*  
*karmabhiḥ preritāḥ sarve bhavābdhau dustare param \|\| 62 \|\|*

62\. О son, who happens to be the father of whom and who happens to be the son of whom; all of them are influenced by their own deeds and are wandering here in the ocean of the universe.

## \|\| 27.63 \|\|

*jñānino mā rudantyeva mā rodīḥ putra sāmpratam \|*  
*rodanāśruprapatanānmṛtānāṃ narakaṃ dhuvam \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. О son, the intellectuals do not lament like this. Therefore, you stop crying because the tears that fall with the crying drive the dead person to the hell.

## \|\| 27.64-66 \|\|

*saṃketākhyoccāraṇena yadrudanti ca bāndhavāḥ \|*  
*śatavarṣaṃ ruditvā taṃ prāpnuvanti na niścitam \|*  
*pārthivāṃśaṃ ca pṛthivī gṛhṇātyasthitvacādikam \|\| 64 \|\|*

*toyāṃśaṃ ca tathā toyaṃ śūnyāṃśaṃ gaganaṃ tathā \|*  
*vāyvaṃśaṃ ca tathā vāyustejastejoṃśakaṃ tathā \|\| 65 \|\|*

*sarve vilīnāḥ sarveṣu ko vā''yāsyati rodanāt \|*  
*nāmaśrutiyaśaḥkarma kathāmātrāvaśeṣitaḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

64-66. By calling whose name the sages cry, they cannot get him back even when they cry for him. This is certain because the soul is part of the body. The skin and bones are consumed by the earth. Similarly a part of the body is consumed by the water, the void goes to the sky and the part of the wind merges into the wind and the part of lustre merges into the light.

## \|\| 27.67 \|\|

*vedoktaṃ caiva yatkarma kuru tatpāralaukikam \|*  
*sa ca bandhuḥ sa putraśca paralokahitāya yaḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

67\. Thus everything merges into the five elements. Therefore no one would gain anything by lamenting. Thereafter, only the name his glory, his good deeds are remembered by the people after the death of a person. Therefore, you perform the last rights as prescribed in the Vedas.

## \|\| 27.68 \|\|

*bhṛgostadvacanaṃ śrutvā śokaṃ tatyāja tatkṣaṇam \|*  
*reṇukā ca mahāsādhvī taṃ vastumupacakrame \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. Because the one who performs these rites for the welfare of the departed soul, he could be called the real relative." On hearing the words of Bhrigu, the chaste Renuka shed all her grief and started speaking to him.

## Chapter 28

## \|\| 28.1-2 \|\|

*reṇukovāca \|*  
*brahmannanugamiṣyāmi prāṇanāthasya sāmpratam \|*  
*ṛtoścaturthadivase mṛto'yaṃ cādya mānadaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

*karttavyā kā vyavasthā'tra vada vedavidāṃ vara \|*  
*tvamāgato me sahasā puṇyena katijanmanām \|\| 2 \|\|*

1-2. Renuka said – I shall follow the footsteps of my husband, but this is the fourth day of the period of my menses when my husband has been killed. О best of those well-versed in the Vedas, because of the merits of many of my earlier births, you have arrived here and you kindly tell me as to what I should do in my present condition.

## \|\| 28.3-4 \|\|

*bhṛguruvāca \|*  
*aho puṇyavato bharturanugaccha mahāsati \|*  
*caturthadivasaṃ śuddhaṃ svāminaḥ sarvakarmasu \|\| 3 \|\|*

*śuddhā bharttuścaturthe'hni na śuddhā daivapitryayoḥ \|*  
*daive karmaṇi pitrye ca pañcame'hni viśuddhyati \|\| 4 \|\|*

3-4. Bhrigu said – О chaste lady, you follow the footsteps of your virtuous husband because on the fourth day of the period a woman gets purified in all respects and can perform all the deeds for the husband. But the woman gets purified on the fourth day for the husband and not for performing divine actions. For performing the tasks of the gods and the manes she is purified on the fifth day.

## \|\| 28.5 \|\|

*vyālagrāhī yathā vyālaṃ bilāduddharate balāt \|*  
*tadvatsvāminamādāya sādhvī svargaṃ prayāti ca \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. As the snake-charmer catches the snake from the hole in the earth, similarly the wife carries the husband with her in the heaven.

## \|\| 28.6 \|\|

*modate svāminā tatra yāvadindrāścaturdaśa \|*  
*ata ūrdhvaṃ karmabhogaṃ bhuṅkṣva sādhvi śubhāśubham \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. О virtuous one, she remains there for the period is equivalent to the life of fourteen Indras. Therefore you also enjoy the result of your good or bad deeds.

## \|\| 28.7 \|\|

*sa putro bhaktidātā yaḥ sā ca strī yā'nugacchati \|*  
*sa bandhurdānadātā yaḥ sa śiṣyo gurumarcayet \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. О daughter, only that son is virtuous who bestows the devotion of the lord and only that woman is virtuous who follows the footsteps of the husband. Only such of the brothers could be termed as true brothers who give away in charity. A true pupil always respects his brother.

## \|\| 28.8 \|\|

*so'bhīṣṭadevo yo rakṣetsa rājā pālayetprajāḥ \|*  
*sa ca svāmī priyāṃ dharme matiṃ dātumiheśvaraḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Only he could be termed as the true family god who protects his devotees, only that king could be called a true king who maintains his people well. Only such a lord could be treated as a true lord who can make his wives devoted the dharma.

## \|\| 28.9 \|\|

*sa gururdharmadātā yo haribhaktipradāyakaḥ \|*  
*ete praśaṃsyā vedeṣu purāṇeṣu ca niścitam \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Only such of the teacher could be termed as true teacher who could bestow on his pupils the devotion of lord. This is because they have been praised accordingly in the Vedas and Puranas.

## \|\| 28.10 \|\|

*reṇukovāca \|*  
*gantuṃ svasvāminā sārddhaṃ kā śaktā bhārate mune \|*  
*kā vā'pyaśaktā nārīṣu tanme brūhi tapodhana \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Renuka said – О sage, О virtuous one, which are the ladies in the land of Bharata who are competent to follow the footsteps of their husbands and those who are considered to be incompetent. You tell me.

## \|\| 28.11-13 \|\|

*bhṛguruvāca \|*  
*bālāpatyāśca garbhiṇyo hyadṛṣṭaṛtavastathā \|*  
*rajasvalā ca kulaṭā galitavyādhisaṃyutā \|\| 11 \|\|*

*patisevāvihīnā yā hyabhaktā kaṭubhāṣiṇī \|*  
*etā gacchanti ceddaivānna kāntaṃ prāpnuvanti tāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

*saṃskṛtāgniṃ puro dattvā citāsu śayitaṃ patim \|*  
*kāntāstamanugacchanti kāntāścetprāpnuvanti tāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

11-13. Bhrigu said – The ladies having small children, those who are pregnant, the lady who has not started the menses, the lady in period, degraded woman, woman suffering from leprosy, the women who are averse to serving the husbands, undevoted women and the women with foul tongue are not considered to be suitable for following the footsteps of their husband. In case by the move of destiny, they do so, they cannot achieve for their husbands the desired goal. The one who pleasantly carries the body of the husband to the pyre and follows him, the one who is dear to the husband, she surely gets him the heaven.

## \|\| 28.14-15 \|\|

*anugacchanti yāḥ kāntaṃ tameva prāpnuvanti tāḥ \|*  
*sārddhaṃ kṛtvā puṇyabhogaṃ divi janmani janmani \|\| 14 \|\|*

*iyaṃ te kathitā sādhvi vyavasthā gṛhiṇāṃ dhruvam \|*  
*tīrthe jñānamṛtānāṃ ca vaiṣṇavānāṃ gatiṃ śṛṇu \|\| 15 \|\|*

14-15. Because such of the ladies who follow their husbands, they achieve them again and they get together again and again in their future births. О virtuous lady, I have narrated to you the position of the house-holder. Now I tell you the position of such of the Vaishnavas as die in the sacred places. You listen to me.

## \|\| 28.16 \|\|

*yā sādhvī vaiṣṇavaṃ kāntaṃ yatra yatrānugacchati \|*  
*prayāti svāminā sārddhaṃ vaikuṇṭhe harisannidhim \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Wherever the husband of a chaste lady goes, she follows him. She ultimately reaches Vaikunt ha together with the husband.

## \|\| 28.17-18 \|\|

*viśeṣo nāsti bhaktānāṃ tīrthe vā'nyatra nārada \|*  
*maraṇena phalaṃ tulyaṃ muktānāṃ kṛṣṇabhāvinām \|\| 17 \|\|*

*tayoḥ pāto nāsti tasmānmahati pralayaṃ sati \|*  
*nārāyaṇaṃ taṃ bhajeta pumānstrī kamalālayām \|\| 18 \|\|*

17-18. But, О Narada, in the land of Bharata there is nothing special if one dies at a sacred place because the devotees of lord Krishna are always free from the death; therefore they earn the same merit by meeting with their end anywhere. They do not fall even at the time of dissolution. Therefore the husband and wife always adore Narayana and Lakshmi.

## \|\| 28.19 \|\|

*tīrthe jñānamṛtaścāpi vaikuṇṭhaṃ yāti niścitam \|*  
*sabhāryo modate tatra yāvadvai brahmaṇāṃ śatam \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. If one dies at a sacred place possessing all the knowledge, he surely goes to Vaikuntha and he remains there up to the life of Brahma with pleasure."

## \|\| 28.20 \|\|

*ityuktvā reṇukāṃ tatra jāmadagnyamuvāca ha \|*  
*vedoktaṃ vacanaṃ sarvaṃ sa bhṛguḥ samayocitam \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. After thus speaking to Renuka, Bhrigu also spoke to Parashurama the words which were appropriate to the time and also according to the Vedas.

## \|\| 28.21-22 \|\|

*ehi vatsa mahābhāga tyaja śokamamaṅgalam \|*  
*uttānaṃ kuru tātaṃ ca dakṣiṇāśirasaṃ bhṛgo \|\| 21 \|\|*

*vastrayajñopavītaṃ ca nūtanaṃ paridhāpaya \|*  
*anaśrunayano bhūtvā santiṣṭhandakṣiṇāmukhaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

21-22. He said, "O Parashurama, О son, you come here. О virtuous one, you relieve yourself from the inauspicious grief and you place the body of your father on the pyre with the head towards the southern direction. You clad it in a new cloth and yajnopavlta but your tears would not fall while doing so. You also remain facing the south.

## \|\| 28.23-29 \|\|

*araṇīsaṃbhavāgniṃ ca gṛhāṇa prītipūrvakam \|*  
*pṛthivyāṃ yāni tīrthāni sarveṣāṃ smaraṇaṃ kuru \|\| 23 \|\|*

*gayādīni ca tīrthāni ye ca puṇyāḥ śiloccayāḥ \|*  
*kurukṣetraṃ ca gaṅgāṃ ca yamunāṃ ca saridvarām \|\| 24 \|\|*

*kauśikīṃ candrabhāgāṃ ca sarvapāpapraṇāśinīm \|*  
*gaṇḍakīmatha kāśīṃ ca panasāṃ sarayūṃ tathā \|\| 25 \|\|*

*puṣpabhadrāṃ ca bhadrāṃ ca narmadāṃ ca sarasvatīm \|*  
*godāvarīṃ ca kāverīṃ svarṇarekhāṃ ca puṣkaram \|\| 26 \|\|*

*raivataṃ ca varāhaṃ ca śrīśailaṃ gandhamādanam \|*  
*himālayaṃ ca kailāsaṃ sumeruṃ ratnaparvatam \|\| 27 \|\|*

*iti śrībrahmavaivartte mahāpurāṇe tṛtīye gaṇapatikhaṇḍe nāradanārāyaṇasaṃvāde bhṛgorbrahmalokagamane brahmoktopāyavarṇanaṃ nāmāṣṭāviṃśo'dhyāyaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

*candanāgarukastūrīsugandhikusumaṃ tathā \|*  
*pradāya vāsasā''cchādya sthāpayainaṃ citopari \|\| 29 \|\|*

23-29. You ignite the fire from the wood and remember all the sacred places of the earth which include Gaya and other sacred places like the sacred mountain, besides Kuruksetra, Ganga, Yamuna, the best of rivers, Kausiki, Chandrabhaga which removes all the sins, GandakI, Kasi, Panasa, Sarayu, Puspabhadra, Bhadra, Narmada, Sarasvati, Godavari, Kaverl, Svamarekha, Puskara, Raivata, Varaha, Srisaila, Gandhamadana, Himalaya, Kailasa, Ratna-parvata, Sumeru, Varanasi, Prayaga, the sacred Vrindavana, the Haridvara and Badrikashrama. You recite the name of the these again and again. And fragrant flower on the fire covered the same with the cloth.

## \|\| 28.30 \|\|

*karṇākṣināsikāsye tvaṃ śalākāṃ ca hiraṇmayīm \|*  
*kṛtvā nirmanthanaṃ tāta viprebhyo dehi sādaram \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. О son, adoring your ears, eyes, nose and face with gold, you give it away to the Brahmana.

## \|\| 28.31-32 \|\|

*satilaṃ tāmrapātraṃ ca dhenuṃ ca rajataṃ tathā \|*  
*sadakṣiṇaṃ suvarṇaṃ ca dattvā'gniṃ dehyakātaraḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

*oṃ kṛtvā duṣkṛtaṃ karmma jānatā vā'pyajānatā \|*  
*mṛtyukālavaśaṃ prāpya naraṃ pañcatvamāgatam \|\| 32 \|\|*

31-32. The copper vase filled with sea-same seeds, the cows and silver, should be given in charity together with daksina in gold. Thereafter, the fire should be lit in the pyre and you should speak out that knowingly or unknowingly, by performing good or bad deeds, the human has met with the death.

## \|\| 28.33-34 \|\|

*dharmādharmasamāyuktaṃ lobhamohasamāvṛtam \|*  
*daha sarvāṇi gātrāṇi divyām̐llokānsa gacchatu \|\| 33 \|\|*

*imaṃ mantraṃ paṭhitvā tu tātaṃ kṛtvā pradakṣiṇam \|*  
*mantreṇānena dehyagniṃ janakāya hariṃ smaran \|\| 34 \|\|*

33-34. Thereafter you bum all the limbs of the body which are free from dharma or adharma, greed or desire which will enable it to proceed to the divine abode of the lord. By reciting a mantra you should circumambulate the pyre and by reciting the mantra you perform all the last rites.

## \|\| 28.35 \|\|

*oṃ asmatkule tvaṃ jāto'si tvadīyo jāyatāṃ punaḥ \|*  
*asau svargāya lokāya svāheti vada sāmpratam \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Reciting ... you must think that you can be reborn in this race once again. You again recite the word svaha and desire for the proceeding of the soul to the region of heaven.

## \|\| 28.36-37 \|\|

*agniṃ dehi śiraḥsthāne he bhṛgo bhrātṛbhiḥ saha \|*  
*taccakāra bhṛguḥ sarvaṃ sagotrairājñayā bhṛgoḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

*atha putraṃ reṇukā sā kṛtvā tatra svavakṣasi \|*  
*uvāca kiṃcidvacanaṃ pariṇāmasukhāvaham \|\| 37 \|\|*

36-37. О son of Bhrigu, you ignite the fire towards the head of the body together with your brothers." Thus with the command of Bhrigu he performed the last rites with his kith and kin. Thereafter Renuka embracing her son Paras'urama spoke to him the pleasant words.

## \|\| 28.38 \|\|

*avirodho bhavābdhau ca sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalam \|*  
*virodho nāśabījaṃ ca sarvopadravakāraṇam \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. "The best form of prosperity in the universe is not to come into conflict with anyone. The conflict is the cause of destruction and upheavals.

## \|\| 28.39 \|\|

*akartavyo virodho vai dāruṇaiḥ kṣatriyaiḥ saha \|*  
*pratijñā caiva karttavyā madīye vacane śrute \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Therefore you do not pick up controversy with the Kshatriyas. And for this you give me your solemn words. You listen to my few other words.

## \|\| 28.40 \|\|

*ālocya brahmaṇā sārddhaṃ bhṛguṇā divyamantriṇā \|*  
*yathocitaṃ ca kartavyaṃ sadbhirālocanaṃ śubham \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Consulting Brahma and your divine courtier Bhrigu, you act according to their advice because it is always good to consult the noble people."

## \|\| 28.41 \|\|

*ityuktvā taṃ parityajya kāntaṃ kṛtvā svavakṣasi \|*  
*sā suṣvāpa citāyāṃ ca paśyantī taṃ harismṛtiḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Thus speaking she left Parashurama and taking the body of her husband in her lap. She entered the funeral pyre.

## \|\| 28.42 \|\|

*vahniṃ dadau citāyāṃ ca sa rāmo bhrātṛbhiḥ saha \|*  
*bhrātṛbhiḥ pitṛśiṣyaiśca sārddhaṃ sa vilalāpa ca \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Thereafter Parashurama together with his brother ignited the fire in the pyre. He then started lamenting with his brothers and the pupils of his father.

## \|\| 28.43-46 \|\|

*rāma rāmeti rāmeti vākyamuccāryya sā satī \|*  
*purastājjāmadagnyasya bhasmībhūtā babhūva sā \|\| 43 \|\|*

*bharturnāma samākarṇya tatrājagmurhareścarāḥ \|*  
*rathasthāḥ śyāmavarṇāśca sarve cārucaturbhujāḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

*śaṅkhacakragadāpadmadhāriṇo vanamālinaḥ \|*  
*kirīṭinaḥ kuṇḍalinaḥ pītakauśeyavāsasaḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

*rathe kṛtvā reṇukāṃ tāṃ gatvā te brahmaṇaḥ padam \|*  
*jamadagniṃ samādāya prajagmurharisannidhim \|\| 46 \|\|*

43-46. The chaste Renuka reciting the name of Rama was reduced to ashes. On hearing the name of their lord, the messengers arrived there at once who were of dark complexion having four arms and holding satnkha, cakra, gada and padma in the four hands wearing a long garland of forest flowers round the neck and the kirita on the head and kundalas in the ears. They were clad in lower yellow garment. Those messengers carried Renuka and Jamadagni to the heaven before the lord.

## \|\| 28.47 \|\|

*tau dampatī ca vaikuṇṭhe tasthaturharisannidhau \|*  
*kṛtvā dāsyaṃ hareḥ śaśvatsarvamaṅgalamaṅgalam \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. Thus the divine couple arrived in the Vaikunt ha and started serving the lord as his slaves with devotion.

## \|\| 28.48-51 \|\|

*atha rāmo brāhmaṇaiśca bhṛguṇā saha nārada \|*  
*pitroḥ śeṣakriyāṃ kṛtvā brāhmaṇebhyo dhanaṃ dadau \|\| 48 \|\|*

*gobhūhiraṇyavāsāṃsi divyaśayyāṃ manoramām \|*  
*suvarṇādhārasahitāṃ jalamannaṃ ca candanam \|\| 49 \|\|*

*ratnadīpaṃ raupyaśailaṃ suvarṇāsanamuttamam \|*  
*suvarṇādhārasahitaṃ tāmbūlaṃ ca suvāsitam \|\| 50 \|\|*

*chatraṃ ca pāduke caiva phalaṃ mālyaṃ manoharam \|*  
*phalaṃ mūlādikaṃ caiva miṣṭānnaṃ ca manoharam \|*  
*brāhmaṇebhyo dhanaṃ dattvā brahmalokaṃ jagāma saḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

48-51. О Narada, thereafter Parashurama accompanied by Bhrigu and other Brahmanas performed the last rites of his parents and gave away riches in charity to the Brahmanas which included cows, land, gold, garments, beautiful beds, water, fruits, sandal-paste, lamp of gems, heaps of silver and gold, golden seat, fragrant betel, umbrella, sandals, rosary, roots and fruits and tasteful sweets. Thus giving away charities to the Brahmanas, he left for Brahmaloka.

## \|\| 28.52 \|\|

*dadarśa brahmalokaṃ sa śātakumbhavinirmitam \|*  
*svarṇaprākārasaṃyuktaṃ svarṇastambhairvibhūṣitam \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. Reaching there he saw Brahmaloka which was built in gold and was having the boundary wall of gold and pillars of gold.

## \|\| 28.53 \|\|

*dadarśa tatra brahmāṇaṃ jvalantaṃ brahmatejasā \|*  
*ratnasiṃhāsanasthaṃ ca ratnabhūṣaṇabhūṣitam \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. He found Brahma there possessing divine lustre and seated on a gem-studded lion-throne in a comfortable posture and adorned with gem-studded ornaments.

## \|\| 28.54 \|\|

*siddhaistapaścāndrāyaṇairṛṣīndraiḥ pariveṣṭitam \|*  
*vidyādharīṇāṃ nṛtyaṃ ca paśyantaṃ sasmitaṃ mudā \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. He happens to be the best of the siddhas, sages, ascetics and was surrounded by them, wearing a serene smile on his face. He witnessed the dance of Vidyadharis.

## \|\| 28.55-57 \|\|

*saṃgītamupaśṛṇvantaṃ gīyamānaṃ ca gāyakaiḥ \|*  
*candanāgarukastūrīkuṃkumena virājitam \|\| 55 \|\|*

*tapasāṃ phaladātāraṃ dātāraṃ sarvasampadām \|*  
*dhātāraṃ sarvajagatāṃ kartāraṃ ceśvaraṃ param \|\| 56 \|\|*

*paripūrṇatamaṃ brahma japantaṃ kṛṣṇamīśvaram \|*  
*guhyayogaṃ pravocantaṃ pṛcchantaṃ śiṣyamaṇḍalam \|\| 57 \|\|*

55-57. He was listening to the singing accompanied by music. The fragrance of sandal-wood, kastQri, saffron pervaded the place. He happened to be the one who granted the results of all the deeds and riches. He was the creator of the entire universe and its preserver as well. He was the supreme lord, complete in all respects, eternal Brahman and was reciting the name of lord Krishna and was revealing the truth about the secret yogic practices when asked by his pupils to do so.

## \|\| 28.58 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā tamavyayaṃ bhaktyā praṇanāma bhṛguḥ puraḥ \|*  
*uccaiśca rodanaṃ kṛtvā svavṛttāntamuvāca ha \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. Finding such a type of Brahman before him, Parashurama stood before him and bowed in reverence to him. Thereafter he started crying aloud and told him the cause of his mental agony.

## \|\| 28.59 \|\|

*bhṛguruvāca \|*  
*brahmaṃstvadvaṃśajāto'haṃ jamadagnisuto vidhe \|*  
*pitāmahastvamasmākaṃ sarvajñaṃ kathayāmi kim \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. Bhrigu said – "O Brahmana, I am born in your race and am the son of Jamadagni. You happen to be my grandfather and are well-aware of everything. What should I speak to you?

## \|\| 28.60 \|\|

*mṛgayāmāgataṃ bhūpaṃ pitā me copavāsinam \|*  
*pāraṇāṃ kārayāmāsa kapilādattavastunā \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. The king who was wandering in the forest feeling hungry having arrived there for hunting, was served with food by my father which emerged from the Kapila cow. He was fed with that food.

## \|\| 28.61 \|\|

*sa rājā kapilālobhātkārtavīryyārjunaḥ svayam \|*  
*ghātayāmāsa mattātamityuktvoccai ruroda saḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. Thereafter the king who happened to be none else than Kartavirya, tempted to snatch away Kapila from my father and in greed for the cow, he killed my father." Thus speaking he started crying aloud.

## \|\| 28.62 \|\|

*nirudhya bāṣpaṃ sa punaruvāca karuṇānidhiḥ \|*  
*mātā me'nugatā sādhvī māṃ vihāya jagadguro \|\| 62 \|\|*

62\. The merciful Parashurama some how controlled himself and stopped the flowing of tears and said to Brahma, "O teacher of the universe, my mother has also left me and accompanied my father.

## \|\| 28.63-64 \|\|

*adhunā'hamanāthaśca tvaṃ me mātā pitā guruḥ \|*  
*kartā pālayitā dātā pāhi māṃ śaraṇāgatam \|\| 63 \|\|*

*āgato'haṃ tava sabhāṃ pramāturmāturājñayā \|*  
*upāyena jagannātha madvairihananaṃ kuru \|\| 64 \|\|*

63-64. I am an orphan at the moment and therefore you are my father, mother and teacher. You are also the performer, the preserver and a man of charity. You, therefore, protect me since I have come to take refuge under you. I have come here in your court with the permission of my mother. О lord of the universe, you tell me the way out by which I should be able to take revenge.

## \|\| 28.65 \|\|

*sa rājā sa ca dharmiṣṭhaḥ sa dayāluryaśaskaraḥ \|*  
*sa pūjyaḥ sa sthiraśrīśca yo dīnaṃ paripālayet \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. Because the king who maintains the downtrodden can be considered as religious, merciful, glorious, respectable and the possessor of imperishable wealth.

## \|\| 28.66 \|\|

*dhanidīnau samaṃ dṛṣṭvā yaḥ prajāṃ na ca pālayet \|*  
*taddehādyāti ruṣṭā śrīḥ sa bhavedbhraṣṭarājyakaḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

66\. Because the rich man who does not care for the down-trodden person, fortune getting annoyed leaves him and he falls from grace."

## \|\| 28.67 \|\|

*śrutvā viprabaṭorvākyaṃ karuṇāsāgaro vidhiḥ \|*  
*dattvā śubhāśiṣaṃ tasmai vāsayāmāsa vakṣasi \|\| 67 \|\|*

67\. Brahma, the ocean of mercy, on hearing the words of the Brahmana boy blessed him at the same time.

## \|\| 28.68 \|\|

*śrutvā bhṛgoḥ pratijñāṃ ca vismitaścaturānanaḥ \|*  
*atīva duṣkarāṃ ghorāṃ bahujīvavighātinīm \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. The four-faced Brahma was astonished to know about the vow of Parashurama, which was quite terrific and could result in the killing of innumerable creatures.

## \|\| 28.69 \|\|

*karmaṇā tadbhavetsarvamiti kṛtvā tu mānase \|*  
*uvāca jāmadagnyaṃ taṃ pariṇāmasukhāvaham \|\| 69 \|\|*

69\. Everything happens according to one's own deeds. Thus thinking he started speaking to Jamadagni, the words which were quite pleasant in result.

## \|\| 28.70 \|\|

*brahmovāca \|*  
*pratijñā duṣkarā vatsa vahujīvavighātinī \|*  
*sṛṣṭireṣā bhagavataḥ sambhavedīśvarecchayā \|\| 70 \|\|*

70\. Brahma said – О son, this is a very difficult vow of yours which will result in the killing of many creatures. The universe is created with the desire of the lord.

## \|\| 28.71 \|\|

*sṛṣṭiḥ sṛṣṭā mayā putra kleśenaiveśvarājñayā \|*  
*sṛṣṭiluptau pratijñā te dāruṇā'karuṇā parā \|\| 71 \|\|*

71\. О son, I have created this universe with great efforts at the command of the lord and your vow is quite horrible and cruel. The universe will be destroyed by it.

## \|\| 28.72-73 \|\|

*trissaptakṛtvo nirbhūpāṃ kartumicchasi medinīm \|*  
*ekakṣattriyadoṣeṇa tajjātiṃ hantumicchasi \|\| 72 \|\|*

*brahmakṣattriyaviṭchūdrairnityā sṛṣṭiścaturvidhaiḥ \|*  
*āvirbhūtā tirobhūtā harereva punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 73 \|\|*

72-73. You want to deprive the earth of the kings twenty-one times. You want to destroy the entire race of Kshatriyas because of the sin of a single person. The universe created at the command of the lord includes Brahmanas, Kshatriyas, Vaisyas and Shudras who always remain in the same status.

## \|\| 28.74 \|\|

*anyathā tvatpratijñā ca bhavitā prāktanena te \|*  
*bahvāyāsena te kāryyasiddhirbhavitumarhati \|\| 74 \|\|*

74\. Because of your traditional beliefs, this vow of yours cannot materialise but you can be successful after making great efforts.

## \|\| 28.75-78 \|\|

*śivalokaṃ gaccha vatsa śaṅkaraṃ śaraṇaṃ vraja \|*  
*pṛthivyāṃ bahavo bhūpāḥ santi śaṅkarakiṅkarāḥ \|\| 75 \|\|*

*vinā'jñayā maheśasya ko vā tānhantumīśvaraḥ \|*  
*bibhrataḥ kavacaṃ divyaṃ śaktervai śaṅkarasya ca \|\| 76 \|\|*

*upāyaṃ kuru yatnena jayabījaṃ śubhāvaham \|*  
*upāyataḥ samārabdhāḥ sarve siddhyantyupakramāḥ \|\| 77 \|\|*

*śrīkṛṣṇamantrakavacagrahaṇaṃ kuru śaṅkarāt \|*  
*durlabhaṃ vaiṣṇavaṃ tejaḥ śaivaṃ śāktaṃ vijeṣyati \|\| 78 \|\|*

75-78. Therefore, О son, you go to Shivaloka and take refuge under lord Shiva. There are several kings who are devoted to lord Shiva who always wear the kavacha of Shiva as well as Durga and till such time as they wear those kavachas no one can kill them without the permission of lord Shiva. You must make great efforts to meet with success, because one could meet with success only by making sustained efforts. You try to receive from Shiva the mantra of Krishna, his kavacha and the Vaishnava glory which is difficult to get. With the application of the same, you will be able to overcome the tejas of Shiva as well as the sakti.

## \|\| 28.79 \|\|

*guruste jagatāṃ nāthaḥ śivo janmani janmani \|*  
*mantro matto na yuktaste yo yuktaḥ sa bhavedvidhiḥ \|\| 79 \|\|*

79\. Shiva, the lord of the universe, happens to be your teacher for many births, therefore, my mantra will be of no use to you. I have spoken to you whatever would be appropriate for you to do.

## \|\| 28.80 \|\|

*karmaṇā labhyate mantraḥ karmaṇā labhyate guruḥ \|*  
*svayamevopatiṣṭhante ye yeṣāṃ teṣu te dhuvam \|\| 80 \|\|*

80\. Because one can get the mantra only because of his deeds and one can get a teacher with his deeds. Therefore everyone gets whatever belongs to him.

## \|\| 28.81 \|\|

*trailokya vijayaṃ nāma gṛhītvā kavacaṃ varam \|*  
*trissaptakṛtvo nirbhūpāṃ kariṣyasi mahīṃ bhṛgo \|\| 81 \|\|*

81\. О Bhrigu, by receiving the best of kavacha named Trailokyavijaya, you can relieve the earth of the Kshatriyas twenty one times.

## \|\| 28.82 \|\|

*divyaṃ pāśupataṃ tubhyaṃ dātā dāsyati śaṃkaraḥ \|*  
*tena dattena mantreṇa kṣatrasaṃghaṃ vijeṣyasi \|\| 82 \|\|*

82\. Lord Shiva can give you the Pasupata weapon and you will be able to destroy Kshatriyas with the use of his mantra.

## Chapter 29

## \|\| 29.1 \|\|

*nārāyaṇa uvāca \|*  
*brahmaṇo vacanaṃ śrutvā praṇamya ca jagadgurum \|*  
*sphītastasmādvaraṃ prāpya śivalokaṃ jagāma saḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Narayana said – On hearing the words of Brahma, Parashurama tfowed in reverence to him and after getting blessed by him, went to Shivaloka with great enthusiasm. This Shivaloka is located a lakh of yojanas above Brahmaloka.

## \|\| 29.2-3 \|\|

*lakṣayojanamūrdhvaṃ ca brahmalokādvilakṣaṇam \|*  
*anirvācyasuśobhāḍhyaṃ vāyvādhāraṃ manoharam \|\| 2 \|\|*

*vaikuṇṭhaṃ dakṣiṇe yasya gaurīlokaśca vāmataḥ \|*  
*yadadho dhuvalokaśca sarvalokātparaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

2-3. This is more astonishing than the abode of Brahma having glory which cannot be explained and is floating in the air besides being quite charming. Vaikuntha is located to its north and the abode of Gauri is located below. The region of the Dhruva is located still below it and it is beyond all the lokas.

## \|\| 29.4 \|\|

*teṣāmūrdhvaṃ ca golokaḥ pañcāśatkoṭiyojanaḥ \|*  
*ata ūrdhvaṃ na lokaśca sarvopari ca sa smṛtaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. The Goloka is located at a distance of fifty crore yojanas and above that there are no other lokas. It is the topmost of all. This has been stated in the scriptures.

## \|\| 29.5-6 \|\|

*manoyāyī sa yogīndraḥ śivalokaṃ dadarśa ha \|*  
*upamānopameyābhyāṃ rahitaṃ mahadadbhutam \|\| 5 \|\|*

*yogīndrāṇāṃ vareṇyaiśca siddhavidyāviśāradaiḥ \|*  
*koṭikalpatapaḥpūtaiḥ puṇyavadbhirniṣevitam \|\| 6 \|\|*

5-6. That Parashurama the best of the yogis who could move with the speed thought reached there is an instance. Shivaloka which was incomparable, immensely astonishing and was the dwelling place of great yogis, siddhas, for crores of kalpas was purified by the auspicious souls.

## \|\| 29.7-13 \|\|

*veṣṭitaṃ kalpavṛkṣāṇāṃ samūhairvāñchitapradaiḥ \|*  
*samūhaiḥ kāmadhenūnāmasaṃkhyānāṃ virājitam \|\| 7 \|\|*

*pārijātatarūṇāṃ ca vanarājivirājitam \|*  
*mudhulubdhamadhūprāṇāṃ maghurvanimohitam \|\| 8 \|\|*

*navallavasamjutam pumskokilarutasrutam \|*
